          <p>The commission may hold meetings and hearings at such time and places as it may designate to accomplish the purposes set forth in this section. It shall select a chair, a vice-chair, and such other officers from its membership as it deems necessary. The commission shall appoint a secretary who shall also serve as a commission member.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.1419.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Gifts, grants, and endowments.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission may accept gifts of money, property, or services; may apply for and accept grants from the United States, the state, a subdivision of the state, or a person for any of its purposes; may enter into an agreement required in connection with it; and may hold, use, and dispose of the money, property, or services in accordance with the terms of the gift, grant, or agreement relating to it. The commission may also make grants, gifts, and bequests of money, property, or services and enter into contracts to carry out the same. The gift acceptance procedures of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.013">16A.013</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.016">16A.016</a> do not apply to this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.1419.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Compensation, supplies, staff.</span></h2>
          <p>Compensation of legislative members of the commission is as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.101">3.101</a>. Compensation of the remaining members is as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.0575">15.0575</a>. The commission may purchase supplies, employ part-time or full-time employees, and do all things reasonably necessary and convenient in carrying out the purposes of this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.1419.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Staff from other agencies.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of transportation shall designate one employee of the Department of Transportation who is an engineer or who has engineering experience and the commissioner of natural resources shall appoint one staff member who shall advise with and assist the commission in carrying out its functions and duties.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.1419.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">National Commission.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission shall be an affiliate of the National Commission and as a member of the National Commission may pay an annual fee for its equal share of the planning program of the National Commission.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.1419.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Program review.</span></h2>
          <p>The commission may review the programs of the various interstate compacts, studies, planning groups and commissions involved in water and land use activities along the Mississippi River in Minnesota.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.1419.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.</h2>
          <p> MS 2018 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/3/#laws.3.140.0">1Sp2019 c 3 art 3 s 140</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.161.1419.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1963/0/875/#laws.0.1.0">1963 c 875 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/1129/#laws.3.1.0">1969 c 1129 art 3 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1971/0/653/#laws.0.1.0">1971 c 653 s 1</a>-5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/35/#laws.0.34.0">1973 c 35 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/166/#laws.0.7.0">1976 c 166 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/260/#laws.0.44.0">1983 c 260 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/285/#laws.0.9.0">1985 c 285 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/312/#laws.1.26.0">1987 c 312 art 1 s 26</a> subd 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/384/#laws.3.2.0">1987 c 384 art 3 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/404/#laws.0.148.0">1987 c 404 s 148</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/337/#laws.0.11.0">1993 c 337 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/407/#laws.0.46.0">1996 c 407 s 46</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/7/#laws.2.24.0">1997 c 7 art 2 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/192/#laws.0.27.0">1997 c 192 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/161/#laws.0.27.0">2001 c 161 s 27</a>,28; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/4/#laws.0.1.0">1Sp2003 c 4 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/23/#laws.0.11.0">1Sp2003 c 23 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/10/#laws.1.27.0">2005 c 10 art 1 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/1/#laws.4.33.0">1Sp2005 c 1 art 4 s 33</a>,34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/148/#laws.2.46.0">2007 c 148 art 2 s 46</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/1/10/#laws.3.32.0">1Sp2011 c 10 art 3 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/142/#laws.3.31.0">2013 c 142 art 3 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/77/#laws.2.51.0">2015 c 77 art 2 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/62/#laws.2.105.0">2023 c 62 art 2 s 105</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.161.142">
        <h1 class="shn">161.142 GREAT RIVER ROAD.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.142.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>MS 1961 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1963/0/875/#laws.0.3.0">1963 c 875 s 3</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.142.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Location; construction; improvement; maintenance; acquisition of land.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of transportation shall establish and locate the route or routes of the Great River Road and shall thereafter construct, reconstruct, improve and may maintain same. The commissioner may acquire by purchase, gift or eminent domain proceedings, in fee or such lesser estate as the commissioner may determine, all lands and properties needed in laying out, establishing, constructing, reconstructing, and improving the Great River Road in Minnesota.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.142.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Inclusion within state system; controlled access.</span></h2>
          <p>The portion of the Great River Road in Minnesota may be part of the state trunk highway system and may be a controlled-access highway.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.142.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Acceptance of federal funds; cooperation with federal agencies.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of transportation may accept any federal funds made available to the state of Minnesota for expenditure on the Great River Road. The commissioner may cooperate with the federal government or any federal agency in the establishment, construction, reconstruction and improvement of the Great River Road to the end that the state will obtain all federal funds available for expenditure on the Great River Road in Minnesota. The commissioner may act as agent for any other department of state, public corporation, or political subdivision of the state in accepting federal aid in their behalf for the purposes expressed in subdivisions 2 to 7, and may distribute any federal aid received by the department to other departments of the state, public corporations or political subdivisions of the state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.142.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Cooperation with other governmental units.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of transportation shall cooperate with other state departments, public corporations and political subdivisions in laying out, constructing, reconstructing and improving and maintaining the Great River Road.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.142.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Expenditures limitation.</span></h2>
          <p>None of the provisions of subdivisions 2 to 7 shall be construed as authorizing the commissioner of transportation to expend trunk highway funds for nontrunk highway purposes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.142.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Preservation of adjacent areas.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of transportation and any political subdivision or public corporation adjacent to the Great River Road or through which the Great River Road passes may acquire by purchase, gift or eminent domain proceedings as provided by law any lands or properties, or interests in lands and properties, lying along the Great River Road as they deem necessary for the purpose of preserving areas of natural scenic beauty, views of lake or riverside areas, historic sites, and such lands as they deem necessary for the purpose of providing recreational and rest areas and facilities in connection therewith including camping and overnight facilities. They may enter into agreements with property owners along the Great River Road providing for restrictions on land uses along such road and providing for compensation therefor. Such agreements may provide that the lands or properties may continue to be used for agricultural, horticultural, forest, grazing, residential, or other purposes not inconsistent with parkway principles and standards approved by the federal government and the Mississippi River Parkway Commission.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.161.142.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/0/411/#laws.0.1.0">1959 c 411 s 1</a>-7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/163/#laws.0.33.0">1976 c 163 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/166/#laws.0.7.0">1976 c 166 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/495/#laws.0.1.0">1978 c 495 s 1</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.161.143"><b>161.143</b> MS 1971 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/34/#laws.0.2.0">1974 c 34 s 2</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.161.144"><b>161.144</b> MS 1971 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/34/#laws.0.2.0">1974 c 34 s 2</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.161.145"><b>161.145</b> MS 1971 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/34/#laws.0.2.0">1974 c 34 s 2</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.161.146"><b>161.146</b> MS 1971 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/34/#laws.0.2.0">1974 c 34 s 2</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.161.147"><b>161.147</b> MS 1971 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/34/#laws.0.2.0">1974 c 34 s 2</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.161.148">
        <h1 class="shn">161.148 GREAT RIVER ROAD, LOCATION OF ROUTE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.148.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Commissioner's responsibility.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of transportation shall designate, establish and locate the Great River Road described in and authorized by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.142">161.142</a>, with the approval of the Mississippi River Parkway Commission and, when the location is not on the trunk highway system, with the approval of the political subdivision having jurisdiction of the road.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.148.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Alternate routes.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of transportation may designate, establish, locate and mark alternate routes of the Great River Road with the approval of the Mississippi River Parkway Commission upon highways of the trunk highway system and when not located upon the trunk highway system with the approval of the political subdivision having jurisdiction of the road.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.148.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Expenditures limitation.</span></h2>
          <p>Funds shall not be expended on any alternate route or routes provided for by subdivision 2 except to the extent of any surplus in federal funds provided for the route designated under subdivision 1 or as provided by agreement with the United States government.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.148.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>MS 1976 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/495/#laws.0.5.0">1978 c 495 s 5</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.161.148.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/34/#laws.0.1.0">1974 c 34 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/166/#laws.0.7.0">1976 c 166 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1978/0/495/#laws.0.2.0">1978 c 495 s 2</a>-4</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.161.15"><b>161.15</b> MS 1957 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/0/500/#laws.6.13.0">1959 c 500 art 6 s 13</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.161.15">
        <h1 class="shn">161.15 SPECIFIC LOCATION; LIMITATION ON DEVIATIONS.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may specifically and definitely locate all of the routes of the trunk highway system, but in so locating same, the commissioner shall not deviate from the starting points or terminals as set forth in the route description; nor shall there be any deviation from the various cities named in the routes through which such routes shall pass.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.161.15.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/0/500/#laws.2.15.0">1959 c 500 art 2 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/123/#laws.5.7.0">1973 c 123 art 5 s 7</a>; 1986 c 444</p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.161.16"><b>161.16</b> MS 1957 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/0/500/#laws.6.13.0">1959 c 500 art 6 s 13</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.161.16">
        <h1 class="shn">161.16 HIGHWAY DESIGNATION, VACATION, REVERSION, SURVEY.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.16.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Temporary trunk highways; reversion.</span></h2>
          <p>Until such time as the commissioner definitely locates and constructs the several routes of the trunk highway system, the commissioner shall select practicable existing roads along the general location of such routes and shall maintain them for the benefit of the traveling public. Such roads shall be known as temporary trunk highways. The road authority which had jurisdiction over such road shall, thereupon, be relieved of responsibilities thereto; provided, however, if the definite location of the route shall be other than the location of the temporary trunk highway, the portion of the temporary locations which is not included in the definite location shall, upon notice of the commissioner, revert to the road authority unless the same lies within the corporate limits of a city, in which case it shall become a street of the city, provided that when the portion of the temporary location, which is not included in the definite location lies within a city having a population of less than 5,000, that portion shall revert to the county if it meets the criteria for a county state-aid highway.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.16.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Designation and location by order.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall by order or orders designate such temporary trunk highways, and on determining the definite location of any trunk highway or portion thereof, the same shall also be designated by order or orders. The definite location of such highway or portion thereof may be in the form of a map or plat showing the lands and interests in lands required for trunk highway purposes. Formal determination or order if by map or plat, shall be certified by the commissioner of transportation on said map or plat. The commissioner may, by similar order or orders, change the definite location of any trunk highway between the fixed termini, as fixed by law, when such changes are necessary in the interest of safety and convenient public travel. The commissioner shall maintain a file of these orders as permanent records.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.16.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Public hearing.</span></h2>
          <p>When the county board of any county requests a public hearing in regard to the definite location or a change in the definite location of any trunk highway within its boundaries, the commissioner shall hold such hearing in such county before making a determination in such matters.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.16.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Reversion or conveyance to another road authority.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If the commissioner makes a change in the definite location of a trunk highway as provided in this section, the portion of the existing road that is no longer a part of the trunk highway by reason of the change and all right, title, and interest of the state in the trunk highway shall revert to the road authority originally charged with the care of that trunk highway unless the commissioner, the road authority originally charged with the care of the trunk highway and the road authority of the political subdivision in which the portion is located agree on another disposition, in which case the reversion is as provided in the agreement. When the reversion is to a county and a portion lies partly within a city of under 5,000 population the entire portion shall revert to the county if it meets the criteria for a county state-aid highway.</p>
          <p>(b) If the portion had its origin as a trunk highway, it shall become a county highway unless it lies within the corporate limits of a city, in which case it shall become a street of the city. When the existing road that is no longer a part of the trunk highway by reason of the change lies within a city of less than 5,000 population, the portion shall revert to the county if the portion meets the criteria for a county state-aid highway. In municipalities of over 5,000 population that portion of the road may revert to the county if the appropriate authorities of the state, county and the various cities through which the route passes so agree. Should any city not agree that the portion of the roadway that passes through it shall revert to county jurisdiction, the portion shall not so revert, although the other portions of the roadway in which agreement has been reached shall revert to county jurisdiction. Notwithstanding the other provisions of this chapter or other applicable laws and rules, the commissioner may convey and quitclaim to a county, city, or other political subdivision all or part of the right-of-way of the existing road that is no longer a part of the trunk highway by reason of the commissioner's order or orders. The conveyance shall be for highway purposes, and the future cost of maintenance, improvement, or reconstruction of the highway and the contribution of that highway to the public highway system is reasonable and proper consideration for the conveyance. This subdivision shall apply to all trunk highways reverted before May 29, 1967.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.16.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Damages due to vacation of former trunk highway.</span></h2>
          <p>Damages occasioned by the vacation of any highway or street that had its origin as a trunk highway, if vacated by the county within one year after the commissioner relinquished jurisdiction thereof, shall be paid by the state out of the trunk highway fund. No award of damages determined by the county shall be made for such vacation without the concurrence of the attorney general, and no action brought to recover damages for such vacation shall be settled or otherwise disposed of without the consent of the attorney general. The attorney general may defend any action brought to recover damages for such vacation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.16.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Vacation.</span></h2>
          <p>When the definite location of any trunk highway takes the place of and serves the same purpose as any portion of an existing road, however established, the commissioner may make an order vacating such portion of the road. A copy of the order shall be served upon the owners and occupants of the lands on which is located the portion of the road so vacated and, if the road terminates at or abuts upon any public water, a copy of the order also shall be served upon the commissioner of natural resources. The notice under this subdivision is for notification purposes only and does not create a right of intervention by the commissioner of natural resources. A copy of the order, together with proof of service, or affidavit of publication if the owners are unknown or reside outside the state, shall be filed with the county auditor of the county in which such lands lie. Any person claiming to be damaged by the vacation may appeal at any time within 30 days after the service of the order to the district court of the county for a determination of damages, by serving notice of the appeal on the commissioner and filing same with proof of service in the office of the court administrator of the district court. The appeal shall be tried in the same manner as an appeal from an award in proceedings in eminent domain.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.16.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Survey of trunk highway centerline.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) When the physical location of a trunk highway centerline will be changed by order of the commissioner and the commissioner is aware that a property description has been written to the centerline, the commissioner shall file with the recorder in the county where the highway is located a survey of the existing centerline prior to changing or removing the trunk highway.</p>
          <p>(b) The survey of the trunk highway centerline must be prepared on four-mil transparent reproducible film or its equivalent. Sheet size must be 22 inches by 34 inches. A border line must be placed one-half inch inside the outer edge of the sheet on the top and bottom 34-inch sides; and the right 22-inch side; and two inches inside the outer edge of the sheet on the left 22-inch side. If a survey of the trunk highway centerline consists of more than one sheet, the sheets must be numbered consecutively. The survey of the trunk highway centerline must include:</p>
          <p>(1) a graphic depiction of the existing trunk highway centerline;</p>
          <p>(2) distances along the centerline, and ties to the corners of the public land survey, expressed in feet and hundredths of a foot. All straight line segments of the plat must be labeled with the length of the line and bearing or azimuth. All curved line segments of the plat must be labeled with the central angle, arc length, and radius length. If any curve is nontangential, the dimensions must include a long chord bearing or azimuth, and must be labeled nontangential;</p>
          <p>(3) a north arrow and directional orientation note;</p>
          <p>(4) a graphics scale along with the label "Scale In Feet";</p>
          <p>(5) the position, description, and ties from the trunk highway centerline to corners of the public land survey;</p>
          <p>(6) identification of the public land survey quarter section or sections, government lot or lots, and the county through which the depicted trunk highway centerline runs; and</p>
          <p>(7) the date of the survey.</p>
          <p>(c) The survey of the trunk highway centerline must be certified by the commissioner of transportation or the commissioner's designated assistant and by a licensed land surveyor.</p>
          <p>(d) Upon submission to the recorder in the county where the depicted trunk highway centerline is located, and upon payment of appropriate fees, the survey of the trunk highway centerline must be filed of record.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.161.16.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/0/500/#laws.2.16.0">1959 c 500 art 2 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/0/436/#laws.0.1.0">1967 c 436 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1967/1/11/#laws.0.1.0">Ex1967 c 11 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/207/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 207 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1969/0/336/#laws.0.1.0">1969 c 336 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/123/#laws.5.7.0">1973 c 123 art 5 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/166/#laws.0.7.0">1976 c 166 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/209/#laws.0.3.0">1981 c 209 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/535/#laws.0.1.0">1984 c 535 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/655/#laws.1.31.0">1984 c 655 art 1 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/248/#laws.0.70.0">1985 c 248 s 70</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/1/3/#laws.1.82.0">1Sp1986 c 3 art 1 s 82</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/183/#laws.0.1.0">1989 c 183 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/230/#laws.0.8.0">1999 c 230 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/168/#laws.0.1.0">2009 c 168 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.161.161"><b>161.161</b> MS 2012 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/227/#laws.1.23.0">2014 c 227 art 1 s 23</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">FINAL ROUTE LAYOUT AND PLAN</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.161.162">
        <h1 class="shn">161.162 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.162.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>The terms in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.162">161.162</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.167">161.167</a> have the meanings given them in this section and section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/160.02">160.02</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.162.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Final layout.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) "Final layout" means geometric layouts and supplemental drawings that show the location, character, dimensions, access, and explanatory information about the highway construction or improvement work being proposed. "Final layout" includes, where applicable, traffic lanes, shoulders, trails, intersections, signals, bridges, approximate right-of-way limits, existing ground line and proposed grade line of the highway, turn lanes, access points and closures, sidewalks, proposed design speed, noise walls, transit considerations, auxiliary lanes, interchange locations, interchange types, sensitive areas, existing right-of-way, traffic volume and turning movements, location of stormwater drainage, location of municipal utilities, project schedule and estimated cost, and the name of the project manager.</p>
          <p>(b) "Final layout" does not include a cost participation agreement. For purposes of this subdivision "cost participation agreement" means a document signed by the commissioner and the governing body of a municipality that states the costs of a highway construction project that will be paid by the municipality.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.162.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Final construction plan.</span></h2>
          <p>"Final construction plan" means the set of technical drawings for the construction or improvement of a trunk highway provided to contractors for bids.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.162.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Governing body.</span></h2>
          <p>"Governing body" means the elected council of a municipality.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.162.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Municipality.</span></h2>
          <p>"Municipality" means a statutory or home rule charter city.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.161.162.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/191/#laws.0.3.0">2001 c 191 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/364/#laws.0.3.0">2002 c 364 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.161.163">
        <h1 class="shn">161.163 HIGHWAY PROJECT REVIEW.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.163.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Projects requiring review.</span></h2>
          <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.162">161.162</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.167">161.167</a> apply only to projects that alter access, increase or reduce highway traffic capacity, or require acquisition of permanent rights-of-way.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.163.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Traffic safety measures.</span></h2>
          <p>Nothing contained in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.162">161.162</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.167">161.167</a> limits the power of the commissioner to regulate traffic or install traffic-control devices or other safety measures on trunk highways located within municipalities regardless of their impact on access or traffic capacity or on the need for additional right-of-way.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.163.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Construction program.</span></h2>
          <p>Nothing contained in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.162">161.162</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.167">161.167</a> limits the commissioner's discretion to determine priority and programming of trunk highway projects.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.161.163.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/191/#laws.0.4.0">2001 c 191 s 4</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.161.164">
        <h1 class="shn">161.164 FINAL LAYOUT APPROVAL PROCESS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.164.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Submission of final layout.</span></h2>
          <p>Before proceeding with the construction, reconstruction, or improvement of any route on the trunk highway system lying within any municipality, the commissioner shall submit to its governing body a final layout and project report covering the purpose, route location, and proposed design of the highway. The final layout must be submitted as part of a report containing any supporting data that the commissioner deems helpful to the governing body in reviewing the final layout submitted. The supporting data must include a good-faith cost estimate of all the costs in which the governing body is expected to participate. The final layout must be submitted before final decisions are reached so that meaningful early input can be obtained from the municipality.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.164.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Governing body action.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Within 15 days of receiving a final layout from the commissioner, the governing body shall schedule a public hearing on the final layout. The governing body shall, within 60 days of receiving a final layout from the commissioner, conduct a public hearing at which the Department of Transportation shall present the final layout for the project. The governing body shall give at least 30 days' notice of the public hearing.</p>
          <p>(b) Within 90 days from the date of the public hearing, the governing body shall approve or disapprove the final layout in writing, as follows:</p>
          <p>(1) If the governing body approves the final layout or does not disapprove the final layout in writing within 90 days, in which case the final layout is deemed to be approved, the commissioner may continue the project development.</p>
          <p>(2) If the final construction plans contain changes in access, traffic capacity, or acquisition of permanent right-of-way from the final layout approved by the governing body, the commissioner shall resubmit the portion of the final construction plans where changes were made to the governing body. The governing body must approve or disapprove the changes, in writing, within 60 days from the date the commissioner submits them.</p>
          <p>(3) If the governing body disapproves the final layout, the commissioner may make modifications requested by the municipality, decide not to proceed with the project, or refer the final layout to an appeal board. The appeal board shall consist of one member appointed by the commissioner, one member appointed by the governing body, and a third member agreed upon by both the commissioner and the governing body. If the commissioner and the governing body cannot agree upon the third member, the chief justice of the supreme court shall appoint a third member within 14 days of the request of the commissioner to appoint the third member.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.164.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Appeal board.</span></h2>
          <p>Within 30 days after referral of the final layout, the appeal board shall hold a hearing at which the commissioner and the governing body may present the case for or against approval of the final layout referred. Not later than 60 days after the hearing, the appeal board shall recommend approval, recommend approval with modifications, or recommend disapproval of the final layout, making additional recommendations consistent with state and federal requirements as it deems appropriate. It shall submit a written report containing its findings and recommendations to the commissioner and the governing body.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.161.164.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/191/#laws.0.5.0">2001 c 191 s 5</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.161.165">
        <h1 class="shn">161.165 COMMISSIONER ACTION; INTERSTATE HIGHWAYS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.165.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>This section applies to interstate highways.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.165.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Action on approved final layout.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If the appeal board recommends approval of the final layout or does not submit its findings and recommendations within 60 days of the hearing, in which case the final layout is deemed approved, the commissioner may prepare substantially similar final construction plans and proceed with the project.</p>
          <p>(b) If the final construction plans change access, traffic capacity, or acquisition of permanent right-of-way from the final layout approved by the appeal board, the commissioner shall submit the portion of the final construction plans that shows the changes, to the governing body for its approval or disapproval under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.164#stat.161.164.2">161.164, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.165.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Action on final layout approved with changes.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If, within 60 days, the appeal board recommends approval of the final layout with modifications, the commissioner may:</p>
          <p>(1) prepare final construction plans with the recommended modifications, notify the governing body, and proceed with the project;</p>
          <p>(2) decide not to proceed with the project; or</p>
          <p>(3) prepare final construction plans substantially similar to the final layout referred to the appeal board, and proceed with the project. The commissioner shall, before proceeding with the project, file a written report with the governing body and the appeal board stating fully the reasons for doing so.</p>
          <p>(b) If the final construction plans contain changes in access or traffic capacity or require additional acquisition of permanent right-of-way from the final layout reviewed by the appeal board or the governing body, the commissioner shall resubmit the portion of the final construction plans that shows the changes, to the governing body for its approval or disapproval under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.164#stat.161.164.2">161.164, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.165.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Action on disapproved final layout.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If, within 60 days, the appeal board recommends disapproval of the final layout, the commissioner may either:</p>
          <p>(1) decide not to proceed with the project; or</p>
          <p>(2) prepare final construction plans substantially similar to the final layout referred to the appeal board, notify the governing body and the appeal board, and proceed with the project. Before proceeding with the project, the commissioner shall file a written report with the governing body and the appeal board stating fully the reasons for doing so.</p>
          <p>(b) If the final construction plans contain changes in access or traffic capacity or require additional acquisition of permanent right-of-way from the final layout reviewed by the appeal board or the governing body, the commissioner shall resubmit the portion of the final construction plans that shows the changes, to the governing body for its approval or disapproval under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.164#stat.161.164.2">161.164, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.165.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Final construction plans issued.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall send a complete set of final construction plans to the municipality at least 45 days before the bid opening for informational purposes.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.161.165.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/191/#laws.0.6.0">2001 c 191 s 6</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.161.166">
        <h1 class="shn">161.166 COMMISSIONER ACTION; OTHER HIGHWAYS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.166.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>This section applies to trunk highways that are not interstate highways.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.166.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Action on approved final layout.</span></h2>
          <p>If the appeal board recommends approval of the final layout or does not submit its findings or recommendations within 60 days of the hearing, in which case the final layout is deemed approved, the commissioner may prepare substantially similar final construction plans and proceed with the project. If the final construction plans change access or traffic capacity or require additional acquisition of right-of-way from the final layout approved by the appeal board, the commissioner shall submit the portion of the final construction plan that shows the changes, to the governing body for its approval or disapproval under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.164#stat.161.164.2">161.164, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.166.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Action on final layout approved with changes.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If the appeal board approves the final layout with modifications, the commissioner may:</p>
          <p>(1) prepare final construction plans including the modifications, notify the governing body, and proceed with the project;</p>
          <p>(2) decide not to proceed with the project; or</p>
          <p>(3) prepare a new final layout and resubmit it to the governing body for approval or disapproval under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.164#stat.161.164.2">161.164, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) If the final construction plans contain changes in access or traffic capacity or require additional acquisition of permanent right-of-way from the final layout reviewed by the appeal board or the governing body, the commissioner shall resubmit the portion of the final construction plans that shows the changes, to the governing body for its approval or disapproval under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.164#stat.161.164.2">161.164, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.166.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Action on disapproved final layout.</span></h2>
          <p>If the appeal board disapproves the final layout, the commissioner may:</p>
          <p>(1) decide not to proceed with the project; or</p>
          <p>(2) prepare a new final layout and submit it to the governing body for approval or disapproval under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.164#stat.161.164.2">161.164, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.166.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Final construction plans issued.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall send a complete set of final construction plans to the municipality at least 45 days before the bid opening for informational purposes.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.161.166.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/191/#laws.0.7.0">2001 c 191 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/83/#laws.1.51.0">2020 c 83 art 1 s 51</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.161.167">
        <h1 class="shn">161.167 REIMBURSEMENT OF EXPENSES.</h1>
        <p>Members of the appeal board shall submit to the commissioner an itemized list of the expenses incurred in disposing of matters presented to them. The appeal board members shall be reimbursed for all reasonable expenses incurred by them in the performance of their duties. The commissioner shall pay these costs out of the trunk highway fund.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.161.167.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/191/#laws.0.8.0">2001 c 191 s 8</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.161.168"><b><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.168">161.168</a></b> MS 2008 [Renumbered <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.475">161.475</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.161.17"><b>161.17</b> MS 1957 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/0/500/#laws.6.13.0">1959 c 500 art 6 s 13</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.161.17"><b>161.17</b> MS 2000 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/191/#laws.0.9.0">2001 c 191 s 9</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.161.171"><b>161.171</b> MS 2000 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/191/#laws.0.9.0">2001 c 191 s 9</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.161.172"><b>161.172</b> MS 2000 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/191/#laws.0.9.0">2001 c 191 s 9</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.161.173"><b>161.173</b> MS 2000 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/191/#laws.0.9.0">2001 c 191 s 9</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.161.174"><b>161.174</b> MS 2000 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/191/#laws.0.9.0">2001 c 191 s 9</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.161.175"><b>161.175</b> MS 2000 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/191/#laws.0.9.0">2001 c 191 s 9</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.161.176"><b>161.176</b> MS 2000 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/191/#laws.0.9.0">2001 c 191 s 9</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.161.177"><b>161.177</b> MS 2000 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/191/#laws.0.9.0">2001 c 191 s 9</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.161.178">
        <h1 class="shn">161.178 TRANSPORTATION GREENHOUSE GAS EMISSIONS IMPACT ASSESSMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.178.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Applicable entity" means the commissioner with respect to a project or portfolio for inclusion in the state transportation improvement program or a metropolitan planning organization with respect to a project or portfolio for inclusion in the appropriate metropolitan transportation improvement program.</p>
          <p>(c) "Assessment" means the impact assessment under this section.</p>
          <p>(d) "Capacity expansion project" means a project for trunk highway construction or reconstruction that:</p>
          <p>(1) is a major highway project, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/174.56#stat.174.56.1">174.56, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (b); and</p>
          <p>(2) adds highway traffic capacity or provides for grade separation of motor vehicle traffic at an intersection, excluding auxiliary lanes with a length of less than 2,500 feet.</p>
          <p>(e) "Greenhouse gas emissions" includes those emissions described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216H.01">216H.01</a>, subdivision 2.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.178.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Project or portfolio assessment.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Prior to inclusion of a project or portfolio in the state transportation improvement program or in a metropolitan transportation improvement program, the applicable entity must perform an impact assessment of the project or portfolio. Following the assessment, the applicable entity must determine if the project or portfolio is proportionally in conformance with:</p>
          <p>(1) the greenhouse gas emissions reduction targets under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/174.01#stat.174.01.3">174.01, subdivision 3</a>; and</p>
          <p>(2) the vehicle miles traveled reduction targets established in the statewide multimodal transportation plan under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/174.03#stat.174.03.1a">174.03, subdivision 1a</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) If the applicable entity determines that the project or portfolio is not in conformance with paragraph (a), the applicable entity must:</p>
          <p>(1) alter the scope or design of the project or any number of projects, add or remove one or more projects from the portfolio, or undertake a combination, and subsequently perform a revised assessment that meets the requirements under this section;</p>
          <p>(2) interlink sufficient impact mitigation as provided in subdivision 4; or</p>
          <p>(3) halt project development and disallow inclusion of the project or portfolio in the appropriate transportation improvement program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.178.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Applicable projects.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this section:</p>
          <p>(1) prior to the date established under paragraph (b), a project or portfolio is a capacity expansion project; and</p>
          <p>(2) on and after the date established under paragraph (b), a project or portfolio is a capacity expansion project or a collection of trunk highway and multimodal projects for a fiscal year and specific region.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner must establish a date to implement impact assessments on the basis of assessing a portfolio or program of projects instead of on a project-by-project basis. The date must be:</p>
          <p>(1) August 1, 2027, which applies to projects that first enter the appropriate transportation improvement program for fiscal year 2031 or a subsequent year; or</p>
          <p>(2) as established by the commissioner, if the commissioner:</p>
          <p>(i) consults with metropolitan planning organizations;</p>
          <p>(ii) prioritizes and makes reasonable efforts to meet the date under clause (1) or an earlier date;</p>
          <p>(iii) determines that the date established under this clause is the earliest practicable in which the necessary models and tools are sufficient for analysis under this section; and</p>
          <p>(iv) submits a notice to the chairs and ranking minority members of the legislative committees and divisions with jurisdiction over transportation policy and finance, which must identify the date established and summarize the efforts under item (ii) and the determination under item (iii).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.178.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Assessment requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner must establish a process to implement the requirements under this section, which includes:</p>
          <p>(1) any necessary policies, procedures, manuals, and technical specifications;</p>
          <p>(2) procedures to perform an impact assessment that provide for the determination under subdivision 2;</p>
          <p>(3) in consultation with the technical advisory committee under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.1782">161.1782</a>, criteria for identification of a capacity expansion project; and</p>
          <p>(4) related data reporting from local units of government on local multimodal transportation systems and local project impacts on greenhouse gas emissions and vehicle miles traveled.</p>
          <p>(b) Analysis under an assessment must include but is not limited to estimates resulting from a project or portfolio for the following:</p>
          <p>(1) greenhouse gas emissions over a period of 20 years;</p>
          <p>(2) a net change in vehicle miles traveled for the affected network; and</p>
          <p>(3) impacts to trunk highways and related impacts to local road systems, on a local, regional, or statewide basis, as appropriate.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.178.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Impact mitigation; interlinking.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) To provide for impact mitigation, the applicable entity must interlink the project or portfolio as provided in this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(b) Impact mitigation is sufficient under subdivision 2, paragraph (b), if the project or portfolio is interlinked to offset actions such that the total greenhouse gas emissions reduction from the offset actions, after accounting for the greenhouse gas emissions otherwise resulting from the project or portfolio, is consistent with meeting the targets specified under subdivision 2, paragraph (a). Each comparison under this paragraph must be performed over equal comparison periods.</p>
          <p>(c) An offset action consists of a project, program, operations modification, or mitigation plan in one or more of the following areas:</p>
          <p>(1) transit expansion, including but not limited to regular route bus, arterial bus rapid transit, highway bus rapid transit, rail transit, and intercity passenger rail;</p>
          <p>(2) transit service improvements, including but not limited to increased service level, transit fare reduction, and transit priority treatments;</p>
          <p>(3) active transportation infrastructure;</p>
          <p>(4) micromobility infrastructure and service, including but not limited to shared vehicle services;</p>
          <p>(5) transportation demand management, including but not limited to vanpool and shared vehicle programs, remote work, and broadband access expansion;</p>
          <p>(6) parking management, including but not limited to parking requirements reduction or elimination and parking cost adjustments;</p>
          <p>(7) land use, including but not limited to residential and other density increases, mixed-use development, and transit-oriented development;</p>
          <p>(8) infrastructure improvements related to traffic operations, including but not limited to roundabouts and reduced conflict intersections;</p>
          <p>(9) natural systems, including but not limited to prairie restoration, reforestation, and urban green space; and</p>
          <p>(10) as specified by the commissioner in the manner provided under paragraph (e).</p>
          <p>(d) An offset action may be identified as interlinked to the project or portfolio if:</p>
          <p>(1) there is a specified project, program, modification, or mitigation plan;</p>
          <p>(2) the necessary funding sources are identified and sufficient amounts are committed;</p>
          <p>(3) the mitigation is localized as provided in subdivision 5; and</p>
          <p>(4) procedures are established to ensure that the mitigation action remains in substantially the same form or a revised form that continues to meet the calculation under paragraph (b).</p>
          <p>(e) The commissioner may authorize additional offset actions under paragraph (c) if:</p>
          <p>(1) the offset action is reviewed and recommended by the technical advisory committee under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.1782">161.1782</a>; and</p>
          <p>(2) the commissioner determines that the offset action is directly related to reduction in the transportation sector of greenhouse gas emissions or vehicle miles traveled.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.178.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Impact mitigation; localization.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An offset action under subdivision 4 must be localized in the following priority order:</p>
          <p>(1) if the offset action is for one project, within or associated with at least one of the communities impacted by the project;</p>
          <p>(2) if clause (1) does not apply or there is not a reasonably feasible location under clause (1), in areas of persistent poverty or historically disadvantaged communities, as measured and defined in federal law, guidance, and notices of funding opportunity;</p>
          <p>(3) if there is not a reasonably feasible location under clauses (1) and (2), in the region of the project or portfolio; or</p>
          <p>(4) if there is not a reasonably feasible location under clauses (1) to (3), on a statewide basis.</p>
          <p>(b) The applicable entity must include an explanation regarding the feasibility and rationale for each mitigation action located under paragraph (a), clauses (2) to (4).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.178.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Public information.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must publish information regarding impact assessments on the department's website. The information must include:</p>
          <p>(1) for each project evaluated separately under this section, identification of the project;</p>
          <p>(2) for each project evaluated separately, a summary that includes an overview of the assessment, the impact determination by the commissioner, and project disposition, including a review of any offset actions;</p>
          <p>(3) for each portfolio of projects, an overview of the projects, the impact determination by the commissioner, and a summary of any offset actions;</p>
          <p>(4) a review of any interpretation of or additions to offset actions under subdivision 4;</p>
          <p>(5) identification of the date established by the commissioner under subdivision 2a, paragraph (b); and</p>
          <p>(6) a summary of the activities of the technical advisory committee under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.1782">161.1782</a>, including but not limited to any findings or recommendations made by the advisory committee.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.178.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Safety and well-being.</span></h2>
          <p>The requirements of this section are in addition to and must not supplant the safety and well-being goals established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/174.01">174.01</a>, subdivision 2, clauses (1) and (2).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.178.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Transportation impact assessment and mitigation account.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A transportation impact assessment and mitigation account is established in the special revenue fund. The account consists of funds provided by law and any other money donated, allotted, transferred, or otherwise provided to the account.</p>
          <p>(b) Money in the account is annually appropriated to the commissioner and must only be expended on activities described or required under this section. In determining expenditures from the account, the commissioner must include prioritization for offset actions interlinked to trunk highway projects that reduce traffic fatalities or severe injuries.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.161.178.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/68/#laws.4.28.0">2023 c 68 art 4 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.3.14.0">2024 c 127 art 3 s 14</a></p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>This section, as added by Laws 2023, chapter 68, article 4, section 28, is effective February 1, 2025. Laws 2023, chapter 68, article 4, section 28, the effective date.</p>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>The amendment to this section by Laws 2024, chapter 127, article 3, section 14, is effective February 1, 2025, except that subdivision 8 is effective July 1, 2024. Laws 2024, chapter 127, article 3, section 14, the effective date.</p>
      <div class="section" id="stat.161.1782">
        <h1 class="shn">161.1782 TRANSPORTATION IMPACT ASSESSMENT; TECHNICAL ADVISORY COMMITTEE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.1782.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this section, the following terms have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Advisory committee" means the technical advisory committee established in this section.</p>
          <p>(c) "Project or portfolio" is as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.178">161.178</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.1782.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must establish a technical advisory committee to assist in implementation review related to the requirements under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.178">161.178</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.1782.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Membership; appointments.</span></h2>
          <p>The advisory committee is composed of the following members:</p>
          <p>(1) one member from the Department of Transportation, appointed by the commissioner of transportation;</p>
          <p>(2) one member from the Pollution Control Agency, appointed by the commissioner of the Pollution Control Agency;</p>
          <p>(3) one member from the Metropolitan Council, appointed by the chair of the Metropolitan Council;</p>
          <p>(4) one member from the Center for Transportation Studies, appointed by the president of the University of Minnesota;</p>
          <p>(5) one member representing metropolitan planning organizations outside the metropolitan area, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/473.121#stat.473.121.2">473.121, subdivision 2</a>, appointed by the Association of Metropolitan Planning Organizations; and</p>
          <p>(6) up to four members who are not employees of the state, with no more than two who are employees of a political subdivision, appointed by the commissioner of transportation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.1782.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Membership; requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) To be eligible for appointment to the advisory committee, an individual must have experience or expertise sufficient to provide assistance in implementation or technical review related to the requirements under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.178">161.178</a>. Each appointing authority must consider appointment of individuals with expertise in travel demand modeling, emissions modeling, traffic forecasting, land use planning, or transportation-related greenhouse gas emissions assessment and analysis. In appointing the members under subdivision 3, clause (6), the commissioner must also consider technical expertise in other relevant areas, which may include but is not limited to public health or natural systems management.</p>
          <p>(b) Members of the advisory committee serve at the pleasure of the appointing authority. Vacancies must be filled by the appointing authority.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.1782.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Duties.</span></h2>
          <p>The advisory committee must assist the commissioner in implementation of the requirements under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.178">161.178</a>, including to:</p>
          <p>(1) perform technical review and validation of processes and methodologies used for impact assessment and impact mitigation;</p>
          <p>(2) review and make recommendations on:</p>
          <p>(i) impact assessment requirements;</p>
          <p>(ii) models and tools for impact assessment;</p>
          <p>(iii) methods to determine sufficiency of impact mitigation;</p>
          <p>(iv) procedures for interlinking a project or portfolio to impact mitigation; and</p>
          <p>(v) reporting and data collection;</p>
          <p>(3) advise on the approach used to determine the area of influence for a project or portfolio for a geographic or transportation network area;</p>
          <p>(4) develop recommendations on any clarifications, modifications, or additions to the offset actions authorized under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/161.178#stat.161.178.4">161.178, subdivision 4</a>; and</p>
          <p>(5) perform other analyses or activities as requested by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.1782.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Administration.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner must provide administrative support to the advisory committee. Upon request, the commissioner must provide information and technical support to the advisory committee.</p>
          <p>(b) Members of the advisory committee are not eligible for compensation under this section.</p>
          <p>(c) The advisory committee is subject to the Minnesota Data Practices Act under chapter 13 and to the Minnesota Open Meeting Law under chapter 13D.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.161.1782.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.3.15.0">2024 c 127 art 3 s 15</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">ACQUISITIONS; DUTIES AND POWERS</h1>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.161.18"><b>161.18</b> MS 1957 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/0/500/#laws.6.13.0">1959 c 500 art 6 s 13</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.161.18">
        <h1 class="shn">161.18 PRIOR EASEMENTS VEST IN STATE.</h1>
        <p>When any road or highway, including any city street or portion thereof, is taken over by the state as a trunk highway, the state as to any such road, street, or highway or portion thereof, without compensation paid therefor, shall be vested with all rights, titles, easements, and appurtenances thereto appertaining, held by or vested in any of the political subdivisions of the state prior to the time such road, street, or highway is taken over by the state.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.161.18.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/0/500/#laws.2.18.0">1959 c 500 art 2 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/123/#laws.5.7.0">1973 c 123 art 5 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.161.19"><b>161.19</b> MS 1957 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/0/500/#laws.6.13.0">1959 c 500 art 6 s 13</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.161.19">
        <h1 class="shn">161.19 CERTAIN RECORDS OBTAINED AND FILED.</h1>
        <p>Upon the written request of the commissioner the court administrator of any court, the auditor of any county, the clerk of any town, or the recorder or clerk of any city shall furnish a copy of the proceedings, documents, and plats, if any, relating to the establishment of any road or the procuring of the right-of-way of any road which has been or may be taken over by the state of Minnesota as a trunk highway. The copy shall be filed in the records of the commissioner and shall be prima facie evidence of the existence of the road as described therein. The legal fee for the copies shall be paid from the trunk highway fund.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.161.19.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/0/500/#laws.2.19.0">1959 c 500 art 2 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/123/#laws.5.7.0">1973 c 123 art 5 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/1/3/#laws.1.82.0">1Sp1986 c 3 art 1 s 82</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.161.20">
        <h1 class="shn">161.20 GENERAL POWERS OF COMMISSIONER.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.20.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Provisions of constitution.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall carry out the provisions of article 14, section 2, of the Constitution of the state of Minnesota.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.20.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Property acquisition; agreements and contracts.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner is authorized:</p>
          <p>(1) to acquire by purchase, gift, or by eminent domain proceedings as provided by law, in fee or such lesser estate as the commissioner deems necessary, all lands and properties necessary in preserving future trunk highway corridors or in laying out, constructing, maintaining, and improving the trunk highway system including recreational vehicle lanes; to locate, construct, reconstruct, improve, and maintain the trunk highway system; to purchase all road material, machinery, tools, and supplies necessary for the construction, maintenance, and improvement thereof; to construct necessary buildings, or rent or acquire by purchase, gift, or condemnation, grounds and buildings necessary for the storing and housing of such material, machinery, tools, and supplies or necessary for office space for employees; to maintain, repair, or remodel such buildings as may be necessary;</p>
          <p>(2) to acquire by purchase, gift, or condemnation, replacement sites for historically significant buildings or structures and to relocate these buildings or structures onto those sites, reconstructing and maintaining them until disposed of through public sale to the highest responsible bidder;</p>
          <p>(3) to make agreements with any county for the relocation or reestablishment, by the county, of section, quarter section, or meander corners originally established by the United States, when such relocation or reestablishment is necessary in order to write land acquisition descriptions or by reason of the construction, reconstruction, improvement, or maintenance of a trunk highway;</p>
          <p>(4) to contract on an equitable basis with railroad companies for the installation and reinstallation of safety devices at trunk highway-railroad grade crossings, and for the construction, reconstruction, and maintenance of bridges and approaches existing or necessary for the separation of grades at railroad and trunk highway intersections; and</p>
          <p>(5) in carrying out duties, to let all necessary contracts in the manner prescribed by law.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The commissioner may make agreements with and cooperate with any governmental authority for the purpose of effectuating the provisions of this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.20.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Trunk highway fund appropriations.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may expend trunk highway funds only for trunk highway purposes. Payment of expenses related to Bureau of Criminal Apprehension laboratory, Explore Minnesota Tourism kiosks, Minnesota Safety Council, driver education programs, Emergency Medical Services Board, Mississippi River Parkway Commission, payments to the Department of Information Technology Services in excess of actual costs incurred for trunk highway purposes, and personnel costs incurred on behalf of the Governor's Office do not further a highway purpose and do not aid in the construction, improvement, or maintenance of the highway system.</p>
          <p class="see_note">[See Note.]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.20.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Debt collection.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall make reasonable and businesslike efforts to collect money owed for licenses, fines, penalties, and permit fees or arising from damages to state-owned property or other causes related to the activities of the Department of Transportation. The commissioner may contract for debt collection services for the purpose of collecting a money judgment or legal indebtedness. The commissioner may enter into an agreement with the commissioner of public safety to use debt collection services authorized by this subdivision when civil penalties relating to the use of highways have been reduced to money judgment. Money received as full or partial payment shall be deposited to the appropriate fund. When money is collected through contracted services, the commissioner may make payment for the service from the money collected. The amount necessary for payment of contractual collection costs is appropriated from the fund in which money so collected is deposited.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.20.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Acceptance of private funds.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.013">16A.013</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/16A.016">16A.016</a>, the commissioner may accept on behalf of the state, gifts, grants, or contributions for purposes pertaining to the activities of the department. Funds received under this subdivision must be deposited in the trunk highway fund and are annually appropriated to the commissioner for the purpose for which they are given.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.161.20.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/0/500/#laws.2.20.0">1959 c 500 art 2 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1965/0/505/#laws.0.1.0">1965 c 505 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1973/0/620/#laws.0.2.0">1973 c 620 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1976/0/2/#laws.0.172.0">1976 c 2 s 172</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/641/#laws.1.3.0">1982 c 641 art 1 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/417/#laws.0.13.0">1984 c 417 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/558/#laws.5.1.0">1984 c 558 art 5 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/0/299/#laws.0.2.0">1985 c 299 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/698/#laws.0.2.0">1988 c 698 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/339/#laws.0.3.0">1991 c 339 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/479/#laws.2.4.0">2000 c 479 art 2 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/364/#laws.0.5.0">2002 c 364 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/19/#laws.2.9.0">1Sp2003 c 19 art 2 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/171/#laws.0.18.0">2004 c 171 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/36/#laws.3.3.0">2009 c 36 art 3 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/117/#laws.3.2.0">2013 c 117 art 3 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/227/#laws.1.4.0">2014 c 227 art 1 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/31/#laws.2.16.0">2021 c 31 art 2 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/5/#laws.4.151.0">1Sp2021 c 5 art 4 s 151</a></p>
      </div>
      <p><b>NOTE: </b>Subdivision 3 is repealed by Laws 2021, First Special Session chapter 5, article 4, section 151, effective July 1, 2025. Laws 2021, First Special Session chapter 5, article 4, section 151, the effective date.</p>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.161.201"><b>161.201</b> MS 2012 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/227/#laws.1.23.0">2014 c 227 art 1 s 23</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.161.202">
        <h1 class="shn">161.202 REPLACEMENT OF PUBLIC LANDS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.202.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this section the following terms shall have the meanings ascribed to them:</p>
          <p>(a) "Public lands" means any lands, except streets, roads, or bridges owned by any subdivision of government, including but not limited to, the property of school districts, however organized, towns, cities, municipalities, counties, and any board or commission of any thereof, and public corporations created by the laws of this state.</p>
          <p>(b) "Affected agency" means any governing body of any subdivision of government which owns public lands, and shall include any agency, board, or commission charged with the administration of such lands.</p>
          <p>(c) "Cost of replacement" means the amount paid by any affected agency to purchase and develop lands to replace public lands acquired for the purpose of constructing or improving trunk highways.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.161.202.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Replacement of acquired public lands.</span></h2>
          <p>Whenever it has been determined that the commissioner of transportation is to acquire any public lands for the construction or improvement of a federally aided state trunk highway, including urban extensions thereof, the commissioner may, and in the case of parks shall, upon the request of the affected agency, authorize the affected agency to replace the same within a reasonable time by gift, purchase, or condemnation if granted the power of eminent domain by law. The replacement lands to be acquired by the affected agency shall be designated in an agreement entered into between any affected agencies and the commissioner.
